ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u7	Mr Giovanardi (PL-Id) Mr President, I am speaking on the order of business on a matter of economics for the Senate, but also for the House. I would like to point out that in the budget law currently being examined by the House an amendment has been tabled, signed by a large number of parliamentarians, with which the database for the registration of the early treatment provisions (DAT), financed with 5 million euros, is established at the Ministry of Health. It is a national database in which every adult is capable of understanding and wanting, in anticipation of a possible future inability to self-determine, expresses his/her will in the matter of health treatments, as well as consent or refusal. After that, the government is delegated how to spend the 5 million euros allocated. President. - We are discussing exactly the same subject. We are examining a bill, already approved by the House, which provides for storage at regional structures. If this amendment, which - as I understand it - was declared admissible, were to pass, we would - on the one hand - have a budget law providing for a national deposit and, on the other hand, a law laid down by this branch of Parliament which provides for 20 regional deposits. In this way we would have regional and national deposits at the same time. I would like to point this out to the Presidency, also because it is a matter of the dignity of Parliament, the House and the Senate, because it seems unbecoming to me to legislate in this way. If this will is expressed by the amendment, it is done immediately to the Senate: regional deposits are abolished, amendments are tabled which provide for the national deposit properly funded. Afterwards, the provision is sent back quickly to the House so that it becomes law without having to wait for the double cross, unless the Government does not want to present later a decree-law to modify what Chamber and Senate have just done. It is a buzzword that is difficult to digest and I do not think it serves the dignity of the House and the Senate to move in this direction either.
ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u8	Senator Giovanardi, the problem was already posed yesterday by Senator Gasparri. The subject is at this time being dealt with in the House and, therefore, it is not within our competence. Obviously, the measure will be returned to the Senate of the Republic and we will deal with it when it comes to our examination.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u18	Mr Quagliarello (FL-Id-PL, PLI)). Mr President, you are absolutely right from a formal point of view. I believe there is room for a political comment, because the situation that has been denounced by the previous Members is at the limit. It objectively denounces that this measure has within it imperfections such for which it is necessary to force the Regulations until they change, using measures that are proceeding in parallel. What happened, Mr President? It's very simple. It was realized that regional registers are a contradiction because, basically, the situation can be verified for which one issues a declaration in Campania and another in Sicily, according to the ancient spirit of the Kingdom of the two Sicilies where there were two capitals, one in Naples and one in Palermo. Of course, this concept can also be repeated for other pre-unitary states. We have now become aware of this situation and have spoken to the House through the financial amendment in question. The fact is that this situation is in conflict with what we are now approving. Mr President, you are absolutely right when you say that this branch of Parliament, from a regulatory point of view, cannot intervene on an amendment which is being examined by the other branch of Parliament. Allow me - and allow the House - to stress a situation that is absurd from a legislative point of view. In fact, when the two measures are adopted, they will be objectively in conflict and a further measure will be needed to harmonize them. [[Applause from the FL Group (Id-PL, PLI).]]

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u22	Hundreds of thousands (LN-Aut). Mr President, I am speaking on Amendment No 1,981. We ask to replace the words: "the decision-making autonomy of the patient" with the following: "the orientations expressed by the patient" because we refer to the Oviedo Convention, which established: The wishes expressed above regarding medical intervention by a patient who, at the time of surgery, is unable to express his/her will will be taken into account. This Convention therefore speaks of "desires," which is different from "dispositions." Therefore, by specifically recalling this Convention, we call for the Senate of the Republic to refer to international conventions and not to invent laws that are in the forefront.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u31	Mr Rizzotti (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, I would ask you to vote in favour of amendment 1,987 because it expresses an important concept for the medical profession. Let us not forget that the doctor, before starting to practice his profession, takes an oath and follows a code of ethics, which in Article 3 states that the doctor's duty is to protect life, physical and mental health and the relief of suffering. It is, therefore, an emphasis on the fact that in the life of the professional doctor there can be no therapeutic aggression. Article 4 states that the doctor, in the exercise of his profession, must adhere to scientific knowledge and be inspired by the ethical values of the profession, assuming as a principle respect for life, physical and psychic health, freedom and dignity of persons and, therefore, must not only subjugate interests, impositions and suggestions of any kind. Article 13: "The prescription of a diagnostic test and/or treatment commits the doctor's direct professional and ethical responsibility. If we want to remove any criminal liability to the doctor and health care professionals, who will not be prosecuted when they interrupt diet and hydration and may incur the crime of incitement to suicide or aid to the person's suicide, this is evidence that it is still a masked euthanasia. I will continue with Article 16: The doctor, even taking into account the patient's wishes, when expressed, must refrain from obstinacy in diagnostic and therapeutic treatments from which a benefit to the health of the patient and/or an improvement in the quality of life cannot be reasonably expected. I could continue with Articles 33 and 35, but I quote in particular Article 38: The doctor must respect, within the autonomy and independence that characterizes the profession, the freely expressed will of the person to care and must act in respect of the dignity, freedom and autonomy of the same person. All this only confirms the fact that this bill is useless. Then there could certainly be a great deal of sharing, as we have tried to do in cooperation with the Health Commission - since Forza Italia has tabled a few amendments and only on the substance - if, as has just been mentioned, the Oviedo Convention and the code of medical ethics were respected. It would suffice to change the word "dispositions" into "declarations" and this bill could pass with some improvements, even if the issue of the register would still remain. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u32	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u33	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u34	FUCKSIA (FL (Id-PL, PLI)). Mr President, I would like to ask for amendment 1,987 to be signed by the entire Federation of Liberty Group and I would also like to take the opportunity to ask the House once again to vote on this amendment, partly because something really strange is happening here. First we demand certifications on certifications to the medical professions and the free professionals and then, at some point, we arrive to establish that someone else has to tell the doctor, formed with a long and certified iter all his life, what he has to do and what he does not have to do; from then on to a software or maybe to an organized robot to translate all this into being (with Industry 4.0 technology would allow this and more) the step is short. It is a short step that does not take us very far, but a long way back, because a law - and here it seems that we forget - does not have as its objective the logic of election or of commercials. The law must respond to specific needs and when, instead of responding to them, it leads to problems or even to deeper injustices than the ones it should heal, because the road is paved for this, it does not do its good service; nor does Parliament that is behind it to approve it. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII and Senator Giovanardi.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u45	Hundreds of thousands (LN-Aut). ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u70	ROMANO (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, I have tabled the amendment to Article 1 which deals with informed consent in paragraph 3. As can be seen from the titration of this bill, both the informed consent and the so-called anticipated provisions are placed within it. If in the context of paragraph 3 it is entirely acceptable that every person has the right to know his or her health conditions, at the end of paragraph 3 it is reported that every person can refuse in whole or in part to receive the information or indicate - and here is the vulnus - the family members or a person of his or her trust who is responsible for receiving them and expressing the consent in his or her place, if the patient wants it. What's the vulnus? The vulnus is that if consent in a subject in full capacity is handed over to the family members, it would result in the body of the law itself creating a contradiction between the information that is given to a family member, the consent that is asked of the family members and the trust that would be single. It follows from this that there is a conflict between the doctor and the patient which, in full capacity - and this is an originality entirely from the legal point of view - delegates the family members to express a consensus: a minimum of logic said of what conflict is created when they are more familiar who must express a consensus. That is why I think it is correct, because of the figure of the trustee who is single, that they are not family members, but only one of them. In fact, always within the same paragraph, I do not know why the legislative semantics contemplates that family members are declined to the plural, but the person of trust is declined to the singular. Then there is another aspect, in the presence of a subject in the full capacity to understand and want, colleagues, which is a very delicate aspect: information can also be given to family members who can translate it - linguistically, conceptually, sensitively and variously - to the patient, but it will always be the same patient, if in full capacity, to express a consensus. It seems to me entirely original, from the legal point of view (and therefore obviously also from the legislative point of view), that information is given to family members and that it will then be the same that will have to express a consensus: no, consent will be given to a family member only when he is unable to act, that is the meaning of the amendment that I have tabled. Otherwise, a completely paradoxical situation would be created where more family members feel entitled to a trust relationship in a conflict situation where everyone would interpret a personal truth mediated for a person who is not able to understand or want or who is not, more correctly, in conditions of ability to act. I would like to put the amendment to the lawyers in particular, because if the amendment is rejected, as I can see from the numbers, more trusts would be created with more consensus and the wishes expressed or wishes expressed by the patient at the time will be the subject of debate by more family members who, for the most varied interests, will be able to decide not what is more right, not what is good, but what will be more appropriate and convenient in this situation. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII and the Senators Anitori and Bignami.]]

Mr Romano wanted to foreshadow the chaos that can be caused. I'd like Senator Romano to listen. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. But it's not mandatory, Senator Gasparri. Gasparri (FI-PdL XVII). Because your arguments have aroused the proper attention of colleagues who have understood (in my opinion, but at a distance I cannot decrypt their statements). You, Senator Romano, have posed a problem that in practice translates into the ballet around the bed of the dying person with family members, who may also have concrete interests (one may or may not be heir and so on). Let us imagine this situation in concrete terms, because the norm is abstract, but in concrete terms the family or family changes a lot. If we do not want to make a trivial speech, there is also a question of interest: in families there are these discussions and you will have happened, if not directly, indirectly. This is the most brutal aspect, when even material interest can lead to saying: In short, he is old, he is sick, he has lived many years: now what should we do? Or there may be the ethical and moral debate that can be produced in family members. We have also seen it in striking cases that have dominated the chronicles. I also happened to find a father and a mother who had different ideas about a situation. So what Senator Romano said is as fundamental as his amendment, but you will reject it, I know that. You're making a crazy law, which will allow family quarrels around the bed. Some will do it for a conviction (because there are people who have a different vision), others might have an interest and we make a law that requires a plurality of contexts: think of the legal disputes - it is the case to say "dead father" - that could occur. What will family do? And if they don't agree, will they vote? And who will vote, direct descendants or indirect descendants? Are adopted children, rightly, like natural children, sisters, cousins and up to what degree? Ascendants or descendants? Who, in this context? Because then it will open in court to the first case of contestation of a child, a brother or a parent who will dispute a decision made by others, a crazy discussion, even with criminal profiles, because in this law you wrote another beastly: that is that the doctor is free from civil and criminal responsibility and you wrote it because you know that you go against medical ethics, first recalled by Senator Rizzotti. So the doctor is exposed to consequences from which he will not be saved with this mention, which will be overwhelmed by judicial interpretations. Well, what will the assembly of family members decide? It is a very serious mistake that must be corrected. Don't just look at the clock, the headline of today's newspapers, March 4 or 8: look at a law that concerns life or death! [[Applause from Senator Bignami]] Who are the family members who decide? What if they disagree with each other? Will the para-euthanasic vote be taken? What will be decided? So this amendment is absolutely... ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u110	Mr Rizzotti (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, Amendment 1,484 calls for the definition of "mental" and "cultural" in relation to the term "conditions" of a patient who subscribes to the informed consent. We know that very often patients do not want to be informed or are unable to understand, if not in very simple terms, what can be a therapeutic path, even more so if they have an inappropriate cultural level to understand such information. Also with regard to informed consent, I would like to mention a small fundamental detail of the measure: informed consent is an act entered into and signed by a person able to understand and want at the time when there is a disease situation in which he resorts to the care of the doctor of a hospital structure. It is therefore current at the time when the patient has to sign it. The early provisions of treatment, on the other hand, are an act that precedes, perhaps decades, a situation in which the person will find himself. Now, I think that, even in order to have a uniformity and a text that is able to be understood, whatever the cultural level of the person signing it, a uniform model would be necessary throughout our country for what concerns informed consent.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u114	ROMANO (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, there are two qualifying aspects of the draft law in our examination of Article 1, paragraph 2, which deals with report and treatment, and with Article 5, which deals with shared planning of care. This means that the assumption is that a relationship and an alliance of care between doctor and patient. However, within the bill there are obvious contradictions that lead from the relationship of care to the relationship of care, i.e. contractualization, which is all the opposite of the relationship of care. It is for this reason, in order also to facilitate the relational and decision-making aspect, subject to information, that I put to your attention amendment 1.1486 on paragraph 4, which reads as follows in its form received from the Chamber: The informed consent, acquired in the manner and with the instruments most appropriate to the condition of the patient, is documented in written form. On the other hand, I propose that the following words be added after the words "is documented": "except for health services involving minimum risks." As a result, if we accentuate this contractualization even more, it will always be a relationship that affects and loses itself in the context of the care relationship. That is, if we accentuate more and more the contractualization - let me use this expression - the patient and the doctor will never be moral friends, even with regard to health activities involving minimal risks. Therefore, in order to facilitate the indirect and, I would say, elliptical possibility of making some changes to the text, since it seems to me that it is not possible from an amending point of view, I propose that the Government accept amendment 1,1486, turning it into an agenda. I repeat, therefore, that the following words should be added to the text: "except for health services involving minimum risks." I am not formally grateful to the Government because, by returning to the Assembly, it gives it dignity, where each of us takes full responsibility for its legislative action. I'm waiting for an answer.

At least the Government assumes the responsibility to say what form consent must take, when the patient is not in a position to do so, and which of the family members or the trusted person who has received the information must express it. This is a fairly clear issue that needs to be resolved, otherwise it is as if we did not make a rule, because we let the interpreter decide what to do. ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u147	SACCONI (AP-CpE-NCD). Mr President, these two amendments, of similar content, are designed to ensure that refusal of treatment or withdrawal of consent given are not conditioned by pressure on the waiver of therapies involving depressive states or psychological changes. The role and significance of consent or dissent to treatment must not be seen as the foundation of medical activity, but as its limit. Meanwhile, medical activity is legitimate and necessary in that it is directed to the integral good of the person, that is to safeguard his life and his psychophysical integrity, as well as to the cure and relief of his suffering. The consent to the treatment is not something that can be opposed to the principle of benefit, but is the conscious participation of the patient in the realization of his or her own good.

The same thing happens very often with cancer patients, to whom we try - even with the shortcomings of our National Health Service - to give psychological support in order to be able to best overcome or live this phase of great depression. Since it is a very critical situation, a patient may decide to ask for, for example, interruption of hydration, nutrition and therapies due to his altered emotional state. Now, I believe that all this should be taken into account especially for treatable diseases. This amendment aims to bring back to the normality of a medical lane, in a medical-patient relationship and in a therapeutic alliance that in this bill are totally trampled on, these fundamental principles.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u157	Gasparri (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, Amendment 1.5006 states a very important point, because Article 1 (5) allows for the waiver of diagnostic tests and health treatments, which the person who is able to act may refuse. However, the amendment adds that refusal does not have any effect when it can result in a real danger of non-existent life: a person who is well and who refuses treatment at a time when he is not in danger of life would in fact expose himself to the danger of life by refusing that treatment. In fact, we therefore want to preclude the misuse of this refusal of health care and treatment that could undergo a transformation, as we fear, when we speak of euthanasic drift of the measure. Amendment 1.5006 therefore states that if a person is not in danger of life, and indeed the refusal of those treatments can expose him to a life-threatening danger, this will should not be taken into account, because it would be a suicidal, self-destructive, self-injuryous will. This is the theme and is an important theme, of which perhaps the schedule of the daily newspaper "La Repubblica" does not take into account, but of which the expiration of logic and life must take into account, because a person could refuse treatment not being in danger and then be in danger because of such refusal. We are therefore in the context of a possible risk of assisted suicide which would be allowed by the absence of the safeguard proposed by this amendment.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u169	SACCONI (AP-CpE-NCD). Mr President, in this regard, it seems to me that we have entered the area of amendment relating to the care of the person, that is to say, to hydration and nutrition, which, although guaranteed in an artificial way, constitute, according to medical doctrine, treatment and not therapies. I would like to remind you that this House a few years ago, with a large majority, I believe 90%, said this. I say this in particular to my colleague, Mr Zanda, who was a signatory to a motion, which I have available and which I could read, in which the Group of the Democratic Party (first signatory to Senator Finocchiaro and second signatory to Senator Zanda and with them many parliamentarians) during the so-called Englaro case had to indicate the criteria of legislative intervention in this matter and among them had to emphasize exactly what I said at the beginning of my speech, namely that hydration and nutrition do not constitute therapies, but care for the person. It is very true that the care of the person, who therefore refers to that necessary humanitarian assistance and to his vital needs, is not unlimited; it is evident, however, that in this regard there must be very particular reasons for the appropriateness of the treatments that must also lead to a waiver in this area, i.e. the effects can be more harmful than the benefits for the person himself. Here you introduce, instead, a novelty very relevant to our order, but not absolute, because creative justice preceded you: creative justice, in that case, came to invent even a pharmacological protocol accompanying the subtraction of food and water, but as is known there are creators of law legibus soli who at that time decided to act in this way. Today we in doing so would make a truly new and original decision for our order, but I believe I can say, in my relative knowledge of the matter, also for medical doctrine. We would also innovate with respect to what this House had decided a few years ago, certainly in another legislature, it would also be said with another Democratic Party, with other ruling groups in that party. I always come back to that point, which I cannot but politically note in this debate: I knew a time when the great leftist party was more cautious about these matters and more attentive to the common feeling of the people and I saw gradually an involution towards, instead, the commonplace of some elitist environments, that is, about the new man and the many ways in which this new man should be built. I wonder what happened from 2009 to today, if the Democratic Party then said that hydration and nutrition are not therapies and today it wants to codify by law that they are therapies and as such renunable, despite the serious suffering that, even in a state of deep sedation, reasonably involve for the person. The only thing that has changed in the Democratic Party is that its ruling group today is more than yesterday done by Catholics, I think I understand a lot, a lot, a lot of adults.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u172	Mr Giovanardi (PL-Id) Mr President, in fact there was a moment - I was there and I lived it - a few years ago, when the Senate lived a dramatic day, full of tension and emotion and I still remember the scream with which Senator Quagliariello gave the news, while we were talking about the same topic we are talking about today, about the death of Eluana Englaro and also expressed heavy judgments on the responsibilities that had led to that horrible death. [[Senator Manconi's comments.]] As I said, he could possibly live a plant life, but he didn't have any kind of pain and was in a situation where thousands of other people are at this time, lovingly treated in institutions that guarantee the dignity of their lives even if they have imperfections. I know that some colleagues are nostalgic about the time, in the other century, when these people were being eliminated for their own good. On the other hand, I am of the idea, given that history repeats itself, that we should not put ourselves again on the plan of determining whether a life is worthwhile or not to be lived. I said and I am convinced that they pass through the loving hands of the nuns, from whom, like inside a placenta, that creature was assisted, and then moved her to a room and killed her, of a horrible death, without feeding her for a few days, did not represent a great advantage. What has happened in five years to provoke such a radical turning point and to open the door to events like those we have experienced? I'm going back to the problem. I am thinking of the thousands of people who are assisted - children, minors and children - in institutions that are in irrecoverable situations from the medical point of view, because there are pathologies that do not make them suffer, as Eluana did not suffer, but that do not give hope. In Belgium, Holland and other European nations are solving the problem as in the past century (see the Groningen Protocol): they suppress them. I'm of the idea that suppressing them brings us back to the barbarism of the past century. This is what is happening in Europe and what this measure opens the door to, so that it can also happen in Italy. It seems to me that this is not a step forward, but a gigantic step back from a conception of life that only applies when it is perfect. Before you're born, if you're Down, you're eliminated. After birth, if you have any aggravating disease, you are eliminated. If you're old, you pour out under certain conditions and your life is not worth living, you get eliminated. This is the scenario for Europe. That's the drift we're going to. The historical drift of the positivism of Northern Europe, made by all German medical science at the end of the 1930s, is not an accident in history. [[Comments from the PD Group]] When those doctors and German culture lent themselves to ride these aberrations with enthusiasm, an entire people followed them and history can be repeated. Of course, in the face of those horrors, international conventions were signed after the war, and they tried to put a stop to it. Today there is the Oviedo Convention, which establishes something that you violate with the measure in question, with regard to the wishes and the strict provisions. [[Applause from Senator Rizzotti]] What do we propose with this amendment? First of all, we propose what Parliament unanimously thought years ago. We have also added, adjuvandum, with the exception of cases where, in terminal patients, they are no longer effective according to clinical appropriateness criteria. In other words, the patient dies because of the disease he has, dies because of the pathology he has, dies sedated without suffering, does not die because he is killed, therefore with passive euthanasia. This is a fundamental point, on which I draw the attention of my colleagues, because when, a few months and years from here, the suppressions, for example, of seriously ill minors, who do not have a hope of complete healing, begin judicially... By the way, there would also be technical aspects, because once the probe can be used, another teaspoon; then what is the instrument that is suppressed? So we reiterate, as other colleagues have said, that hydration and nutrition are not health treatments. Senators Gasparri, Sacconi or Giovanardi do not say this: it is a concept defined in the fundamental international rules of medical science. In this regard, discussions have also been held in the past which have been mentioned, but which I do not want to call back here. I shall conclude, because I believe that the time has elapsed. I really call on you to vote with conviction on Amendment No 1,5008 because we cannot make a moral, ethical, scientific and medical reproach. That's what's going on. That's why we are talking about euthanasic drift, that's why we say that the law done this way is a disaster and it would have been better to rely on the therapeutic alliance between the medical family and the sick. Hydration and nutrition are not a medical treatment, considering them to be such is a serious mistake. This error can be avoided by voting in favour of amendment 1.5008. ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u183	ISGRO CHOLIPOTI' (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, I only speak for a minute. Allow me to speak as a doctor: I heard colleagues talking about nutrition and nutrition. The bill under consideration says that hydration and nutrition must be stopped, accompanying this with a deep sedation. Between nutrition and nutrition there is a difference and I apologize to the doctors present, because they know this reflection much better than I do: nutrition is a set of biological processes that affect survival, while nutrition serves to ensure that metabolism within the organism can take place. The law under consideration provides for the suspension of nutrition, which is different from nutrition. The law says euthanasia is physiological and normal. This law leads to the suspension of hydration and nutrition. Food does nothing but ensure the processes of metabolism within the organism, so when colleagues draw the attention of those present to say that the bill must be seen with great caution and that a careful reflection must be made in the overall interest of everyone, they are not taking a partisan position and against someone, but they are only exercising common sense, which calls us to speak in the Assembly and to tell colleagues that we should be more thoughtful and not be moved by the urgency of adopting a law at all costs, which could put in place irreversible paths towards those who come after us.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u195	ROMANO (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, let us enter into one of the most conflicting issues of this bill. I would like to use a rational, substantial, non-formal or instrumental argument. This is a very important aspect. I would like to start from the reflections made by my colleague Senator Bianco just now when he invoked, in his perspective, the sharing of a law that was mild. There is no mild law in the field of bioright. There's only one right law. It is not the aggression of the mildness of the law that makes the law acceptable for itself, but the law refers to a principle of justice; and there is only one adjective that can be combined with the law, which is precisely that of the right law. Moral friendship, as Engelhardt said, is not merely a formal sharing but is substantial sharing in the light of principles and reference values shared. This is moral friendship. The code of ethics is constantly being invoked and I credit Senator Bianco for having been President of the National Federation of Medical Surgeons and Dentisters (FNOMCeO) when this was approved. But it is that same code of ethics that unequivocally recalls the use of declarations and not provisions. So, the code of ethics, if we want to call him back, let's call him back. However, I want to enter immediately into the heart of this theme, which is very delicate, in which it expresses itself in a strong way (which for me is entirely original) that a law defines what, in the biomedical field, is a treatment and what is not. This is for a very simple reason: anything in medicine is the result of a convention; it is the result of a state of care research that causes the use of a treatment to be classified whether it is a therapeutic treatment or a treatment of care. It follows that if you use this terminology and say that "For the purposes of this law, artificial nutrition and artificial hydration" are considered health treatments, I put this reflection to your attention: every law that specifies something excludes something else. For a sort of logical deductiveism I must draw this conclusion: if, only and only because the law refers exclusively to nutrition and hydration, these are health treatments, I must believe that artificial ventilation is not a health treatment. In fact, I would have expected, within this paragraph, the introduction in this logic also of artificial ventilation. So anyone who will approve this amendment knows that it is going to ratify that artificial ventilation is not a health treatment but is a vital support. I would like to make one more point. It is not the use of a medical prescription or the intervention of healthcare professionals who type treatment as health care. We mistakenly use a synonym between health treatment and treatment: that is absolutely not the case. Just a trivial example: palliative care and pain therapies, which we all know to be of intrinsic ethics that gives rise to sharing, require the intervention of highly specialized health personnel. The palliative care and pain therapies require the intervention of products processed by pharmaceutical companies or laboratories, with the intervention of doctors. You must explain to me, then, of the two, the one: either palliative care and pain therapies are treatments, also these health care, understood as therapeutic, or they cannot be, because they do not carry out a therapeutic action. It is true that the cure contemplates the therapy - topic, this, to me very dear - but it cannot be exhausted in the therapy; the therapy, in fact, is aimed at the healing of a person, while the cure is aimed no longer at the healability of a person, but at the assistance of the person where this is incurable. [[Applause from Senator Giovanardi]] Will you ask me, at this point, if this amendment of mine can meet a better redefinition of what has been referred to in the paragraph? Yes. I ask you to be kind enough to read it with me in the text to your attention. For the purposes of this law, the patient evaluates with the doctor (treatment report) the contexts in which, in the imminence of death (in the imminence of death it is permissible to suspend any treatment, or that we call it health care, or that we call it therapeutic, or that we call it cure), methods of artificial nutrition or hydration still effective, should be considered health treatments. I believe that this amendment puts nutrition and hydration in a perimeter of clinical, relational and ethical correctness. In other words, where nutrition and hydration no longer have any purpose, there is all the lawfulness of being able to suspend them. But when? Only in case of imminence of death. If you would like, then, there is a second amendment, which I would ask the Presidency, please, to be able to explain later, which extends the possibility of extending this type of intervention.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u204	SACCONI (AP-CpE-NCD). We agree with Senator Romano's amendment and we will vote in favour of it. Senator Romano makes some considerations that are not very different from those of Mr Bianco, to listen well to him. Beyond the heated tone used in defense of this text, he is honored to take the floor, which is not usually the case in this debate. However, Mr Bianco, apart from his tone, it seems to me that the care of the person is an important prerequisite for the effectiveness of the therapies. The emphasis he placed on the care and well-being of the person seemed to me to be one more reason for ensuring care; on the contrary, he helped to emphasize the distinction between treatment and treatment, that distinction which Mr Romano spoke of earlier. The treatments are aimed at curing the disease and the care - I say it technically - are aimed at the care that we give to the person in his vital needs. I have not understood how the very consideration of the importance of treatment can lead to a sort of equation for which, precisely because important, they are as renunciable as the therapies. The position of Senator Romano is not very different from motion 87, tabled by representatives of the Democratic Party Group in 2009, which affirmed such a principle. His point 11 stated: "hydration and nutrition, indicated in the different forms in which science and technology can provide them to the patient - even in an artificial way - as physiologically aimed at life support, are not assimilated to therapeutic aggression and are always guaranteed to the patient until the end of life." With regard to this statement, an exception is also admitted which - I think Mr Romano can agree - should be in the sign of that principle of appropriateness, which should preside over any treatment, and which he also intends to renounce by the patient, in agreement with his doctor (the therapeutic alliance returns), at the present and concrete moment in which that will is realized, i.e. not in a predetermined way, but in a current way in a situation of proximity to the conclusion of the life cycle. Therefore, not, for example, in living conditions that could last for years, as happens in the vegetative states, which are defined as persistent because no one knows what the level of their reversibility is. I would like to remind you, among other things, that in many of these cases artificial nutrition is a substitute for a diet that could be carried out directly even if it is difficult, because there is swallowing in many cases, so it is a way of ensuring greater comfort for those who attend and for the same person who is being assisted. But theoretically and also practically it would be possible to feed directly, in about two or three hours, probably, compared to the devices used for the artificial power supply. Here we go again. How can we not see this even more as a grey area? How can we fail to see even more in this case the need for the therapeutic alliance? How can we fail to see even more in this case the need for attention, an affirmation of the principle from which there may then also be derogations entrusted not to a strict rule, but to the will of the patient and the doctor? I would say that here we are precisely in the emblematic context of the right way of law-making that has manifested itself in the law on voluntary termination of pregnancy, in which this criterion has been adopted several times to assert a principle and to allow some limited derogations. In any case - I am sorry Senator Bianco, you know that I esteem you as I admire President De Biasi - I do not think it is a good way to legislate to pursue the creative judiciary. We are not gathered here to give reason to the magistrate, who has anticipated us with sentences that should, apart from the opinion of each one, find us united in affirming the primacy of Parliament and of the people. However, I see that many are now restless with other primates that upset our constitutional balance. [[Applause from Senators Di Giacomo and Liuzzi. Senator Airola's comments.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u211	Mr Rizzotti (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, we have certainly reached the crucial point of the draft law under discussion, namely that of nutrition and hydration, which are considered undesirable therapeutic devices. Probably few in this House - or perhaps I will be the only one - have attended intensive care departments when, independently, according to the person's clinical situation, doctors have decided to suspend hydration or protein intake depending on what happened. In some cases where the clinical situation of the patient requires it, hydration is already normally stopped because important edemas that create pain and suffering, such as cardiac or pulmonary oedema, are created in the imbalances of the body. All the more reason, perhaps, to increase the protein quota precisely to bring back the electrolyte imbalance, so as to reabsorb the edemas. This already happens and on this point we return to the basic error of the bill under consideration and, that is, not to recognize the therapeutic alliance between doctor, patient and family. Probably wanted to get to this law by chasing the network of some or the media conviction and surprised me that probably people who want to approve this measure do not know well in real terms what they are voting for. I was surprised to see in the media the dramatic cases and photographs of the Welby cases or the DJ. However, I would like to remind you that people who are able to understand and want the law already allow them to refuse treatment and treatment. When a patient with amyotrophic lateral sclerosis has a respiratory crisis, the law already allows him to refuse to be put on a ventilator to maintain artificial respiration. So probably such cases have been amplified to get to this. It is clear that removing nutrition and hydration from a person is a sign of masked euthanasia, of which the doctor becomes an accomplice even if he does not want to be, because there is no conscientious objection. I just want it to be clear and there was the courage to say that principle. I would remind you that in the Health Commission - and I hope it will be approved before the end of the parliamentary term - we are examining a draft law, at my first signature, in order to prevent food behavior disorders, with the introduction in the penal code of Article 580- bis which provides for the crime of incitement to suicide against those on the sites, cheering on anorexia, produces death. And just last week Ivrea's attorney denounced a girl who administered one of the 300,000 blogs on this issue, on the report of the mother of a 14-year-old girl. We know perfectly well that removing nutrition and hydration - more hydration, I would say, than nutrition - brings the person to certain death. So, why not say that this is a veiled form of euthanasia? It's reality. Let us have the courage to say that, when a person refuses treatment, there must be sedation that accompanies it, as it already happens every day, to death. It happened - as Senator Gasparri said - in our families, and it happens in hospitals: when a family member is told to bring the patient home, it is because maybe at home he could be more gently accompanied at the end of his suffering. But it's different. That's why I find it pointless to hide behind the form of an act of civilization. This bill is badly done, it is certainly not an act of civilization, but it is the pursuit of a media effect on the skin of suffering people.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u234	ROMANO (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, paragraph 6 of Article 1 addresses the issue of the protection of the doctor when there is a discrepancy between the patient's will and good clinical practice and the guidelines to be followed by the doctor. Also in line with the law on medical responsibility that we recently passed, published in the Official Journal, we know that good clinical practices and guidelines represent a lighthouse, a reference point. And it seems to me that paragraph 6 is in perfect harmony, except to verify, however, that it forgets that there may be an express request from the patient to stop treatments complying with the guidelines and standards of good clinical practice referable to the current condition of the applicant. This is the content of the amendment that I put to the attention of the House: that is not only to put into practice the treatment that might not be in line with the guidelines and good practices, but also to suspend it. This is the content of amendment 1,1793, which, in my opinion, complements in the most correct way the purpose of the provision referred to in paragraph 6. There is a passage that I think is very important, contained in the incipit of paragraph 6, in which it is written: "the doctor is obliged to respect the will expressed by the patient". Let's get on with this. If there is a treatment relationship, the doctor cannot respect absolutely, but he must consider and, together with the patient, make a therapeutic and care path that is in the absolute interest of the patient himself. I want to bring an example of an extreme banality, to make this my concept clearer and be clearer myself. If I, profane of architecture, turn, not by chance, to an archistar, to whom I say that I want to build an auditorium according to certain characteristics decided by me, the latter will tell me that I cannot comply with my will because, if he were to build an auditorium according to what I indicated, this would collapse immediately. In other words, out of metaphor, if there are and we all recognize a relationship and an alliance of care, the doctor must take into consideration and cannot absolutely respect the patient's will. In fact, having to respect absolutely, using harmful treatments, could be counterproductive for the patient himself. We must look at the measure as a whole. Let us not forget that the subscription of the DAT does not contemplate the figure of the doctor, which means that to me, patient (or such future), lacks the dialogue - I say the contradictory - with the competent in order to be able to build a path shared from the point of view of care and care. For this reason, the amendment before us proposes to replace the words 'to respect' by the following: 'to take into account'. This - as has been repeatedly invoked - would be in perfect harmony with the code of ethics, with the Oviedo Convention, various other documents and the dictates of the National Committee for Bioethics, which speaks of a consideration, but not of the imperativeness of the act itself.

It is an anomaly which we have a duty to resolve, which we have probably aggravated in this parliamentary term, but which must always be the key to the reading of our measures at a time when we have not yet resolved it.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u309	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u311	ROMANO (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, we are dealing with Article 2 of the measure: 'Therapy of pain, prohibition of unreasonable obstinacy in care and dignity in the final stage of life'. However, within paragraph 2 of the article there is a confusion of a semantic-biomedical order (and, consequently, also legislative), where the term "suffering" is used as a synonym of the term "pain." The dimension of pain is exquisitely mechanical, but in its reverberation at the level of the lived or cortical, and - therefore - psychic or spiritual, it becomes a experience of suffering. If we were to consider the terms in question - suffering and pain - as synonyms, making a great force of terminological order (from which some results are obtained), it would result that, as reported in the incipit of paragraph 2, in the cases of patients with short-term inauspicious prognosis, the doctor should refrain from any unreasonable obstinacy. I remember that the short term is a classification of a completely non-specific temporal type, which can mean a few weeks, a few months or even more months. Those who have a minimum of familiarity in the field of care know that, once they have made a diagnosis of pathology to inauspicious prognosis, they can then spend quite some time. Colleagues, you will ask me why I do this kind of reflection. The reason is very simple. Paragraph 2 states that, in the face of a diagnosis with short-term inauspicious prognosis, in the presence of sufferings refractory to health treatments, continuous palliative sedation may be used, in combination with pain therapy, with the consent of the patient. Going to the deep palliative sedation continues for a person who has had a short-term infausta prognosis (as I said before, with quite extended time), being this combined, as we unfortunately know, with certain treatments, will inevitably lead to the person's exitus. But it's not enough. The expression "in the presence of sufferings that are refractory to health treatments" is used, which - in order to be extremely strict in terms - I believe it means "in the presence of irreversibly refractory suffering" because refractoryity is either an act of irreversibleity, or it is a data - hic et nunc, here and now - that can find its solution in an anthalgic or pain relief treatment. This also means that if we extend the term "suffering" to other types of pathologies, such as those of a psychiatric order, we would go to justify the use of deep palliative sedation also in situations of suffering of a psychiatric order. In fact, Article 2 refers - in general terms - to prognosis and not to oncologic diseases, nor to neurovegetative states, or chronic or degenerative diseases. It is for this reason that I put to the attention of the House a corrective amendment to the provision, which - once again - is respectful of the dignity of the patient, without conculting its possibility of survival.

I asked for the floor during the examination of paragraph 1 of Article 2, on a matter which is unclear to me and which, perhaps, is also unclear to the House. Paragraph 1 states that "The doctor, using appropriate means in the patient's condition, must endeavour to alleviate the suffering of the patient, even in the event of refusal or withdrawal of consent to the health treatment indicated by the doctor." It is not very clear to me whether the patient, rejecting the doctor's proposal to alleviate his suffering with pain therapy, refuses all kinds of therapy, as has already been said, beginning with hydration and nutrition. In this case, however, should the doctor work anyway, regardless of the patient's will? It all seems very strange to me.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u334	CALIENDO (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, for those who do not have the text in front of them, the amendment provides that informed consent, when it affects the quality of the next life, is expressed by the child who is 16 years old. This proposal came from having participated, years ago, as a magistrate, in a meeting of oncologists at the National Institute of Cancers in Milan, to find a reason on which to determine who should give consent when a minor was the subject of interventions that could affect the subsequent quality of life - let me give an example: the cutting of a leg, of the two legs, the loss of arms - for clinical reasons. The question is: is the child who has turned 16 who, as you know, already has a number of rights possibilities, such as contract marriage, can or must express his consent? Along with all those professors, we felt that the consent of the child who was sixteen was necessary. Some argue that it is also necessary to take account of the position of the child who has reached the age of fourteen in terms of dissent. I have limited myself to the most obvious thing: in predicting that it is parents or others who give consent, do you realize that we have an impact on the quality of life of a child who has already reached the age of sixteen? Do not tell me that this rule must be approved as it is because it cannot go to the House. Do you realize you're doing something of unheard of gravity? Then you talk to me about minors, about the protection of minors, about laws that are not approved for the protection of minors or about biological research: when a child has reached the age of sixteen he has the right to express his consent when it can be drawn from a type of intervention that affects the quality of his later life. You can also vote against this amendment without even discussing it, but each of you who have children and grandchildren, having the opportunity to assess a principle that is of legal civilization as well as of social civilization, cannot wash your hands with the excuse that you have to vote on the measure, because I believe that a sacrifice, even at the last moment, can be made by the other branch of Parliament. [[Applause from Senator Bignami.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u342	Mr Quagliarello (FL-Id-PL, PLI)). Mr President, this amendment, like the next one, deals with informed consent and minors, on which Mr Caliendo was also a little while ago. It states that parents or guardians exercise their country of authority, having as their sole purpose the protection of the psychophysical health and the life of the child. Mr President, this is a very important issue, because this bill deals mainly with two categories of patients: on the one hand, those who are affected by degenerative diseases and, on the other, those who suffer trauma as a result of accidents. If you, Mr President, and your colleagues care to see specialized literature, you would realize that this second category includes mainly young people, sometimes underage, sometimes who are just over the age of majority. What struck me was that these traumatized young people, even because of their physical qualities and abilities, according to literature, when they fall unconscious, in 90 percent of the cases return to perform their vital functions regularly; they resume, let's say, with the current language, one hundred percent. You understand, Mr President, that in such a situation, the issue of informed consent (and that this consent is given in the sole interest of the child's health) is very important. This, in fact, is one of the delicate points. Bad information could cause a severe loss of life, even lives that could have been fully recovered. This, all the more so if it is true what Mr Caliendo says, on which I have doubts; but, if it is true that it is necessary to take account of the opinion of even a sixteen-year-old and, in some way, of his earlier statement, it is even more important that the informed consent be effective and that this be rendered in the exclusive interest of the child's health.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u353	MALAN (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, of course, the article 'Il' is not to be considered, it is a refutation. We call for the words to be deleted: "in full respect for his dignity", not because we do not have full respect for the dignity of the minor who is in this situation but, on the contrary, because this expression is extremely ambiguous. We have seen this in the case of Charlie Gard, the ten-month-old English child whose judges denied the possibility of leaving the hospital to seek innovative care in the United States with the good reason that it could be too stressful for this child to face the journey. The alternative was to die. So, in full respect for life, it has meaning, while with the expression "in full respect for dignity" we do not know where we end up. "Dignity" seems like an ambiguous word, which in that case was used to say no, which was not dignified, it was not appropriate for this child to go on a journey to get out of the hospital: it is better for him to die. This Article 3, in my opinion, is the most important of all the measure, because it concerns minors and children can be both 16 years old or 17 years old, who are fully able to express their opinion on the treatment, especially if they are in conditions such as to deal with them, because it matures very quickly in those circumstances, both children totally without the possibility, even if in full health, to express any consent or refusal. The removal of these words would therefore give an element of clarity. Otherwise, it would be good for us to be told what dignity means, since it is regarded as something different from life.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u388	SACCONI (AP-CpE-NCD). Mr President, paragraph 5 of Article 3 is an emblematic reminder of the Charlie Gard case. It is assumed that there is a conflict between the representative of the poor person who refuses the proposed treatment and the doctor who considers the appropriate and necessary treatment, referring the resolution of the conflict to the judge to protect. Therefore, the decision on the appeal, available both from the representative of the person who refuses treatment, and from the doctor or legal representative of the health facility, would have this outcome. I think it is unreasonable to pass the decision on to a medically uncompetent subject such as the magistrate; pardon, the magistrate is competent in everything and, as is well known, I have also recalled that he has developed original pharmacological protocols, so excuse me if I assume the court's incompetence in this regard. In any case, I do not think that his absolute knowledge can make him compare with a doctor who is able to assess the situation in practice and thus give rise to a composition that takes into account all the elements that contribute to defining it.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u391	Mr Quagliarello (FL-Id-PL, PLI)). Mr President, the amendment I signed proposes the deletion of paragraph 5 of Article 3. This paragraph, as already illustrated by Senator Sacconi, provides that the diatribe, in case of divergence between the legal representative or the director of support of an disabled person and the doctor, who considers that the treatment is appropriate and necessary, is resolved by the judge. We have had an experience not identical, but assimilable, to which reference has already been made, namely the Charlie Gard case. Objectively the worst solution is to refer the decision to the court; it is probably the most inhuman, the one that completely escapes from that concept of therapeutic alliance that here is interpreted in a different way, but that none of us has rejected. In a case like this and if the conflict is of this kind, we believe that the deletion of paragraph 5 would enable the informed, objectively more motivated, judgment of a doctor to prevail. Mr President, it is pointless to tear our clothes apart and perhaps write tweets when these things happen and then, when we are going to legislate on assumptions that somehow remind us of events, we do not even move to try to understand what solutions can come from the experience of actual events. For this reason, I would like to ask my colleagues for a moment's reflection on Amendment 3.5005 and a symbolically greater vote than the votes that the amendments discussed so far have collected.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u405	Mr Giovanardi (PL-Id) Mr President, I would like to know what the Italians who were impressed a few years ago thought about this, when the news came from the United States of a young woman who was sick, who had to die, although her father and mother were struggling desperately in the courts to be entrusted to them, because her husband - from whom she was separated - who was her caretaker, insisted that she die. Parents were not given their daughter. This surprised us very much, infuriated the Italian public opinion, as happened for the vast majority of Italians in the case of the little Charlie of London; not only did they get outraged, but they were surprised, wondering why this could have happened, despite the parents imagining some possible therapy able to give hope to their son. This morning there was another case, reported in the newspaper "The Truth" of parents fighting because in Italy they found a hospital structure that offered to check if there are the possibilities of an improvement for their child. Why does he have to die? Why is it a court that decides that the child must be suppressed against the will of the parents? Surely the majority of Italians think so. The problem is that something totally different is written in the law on our examination. I read it so that there is clarity: the court intervenes in the event that the legal representative of the minor refuses the treatments proposed and the doctor considers that they are appropriate and necessary. It is the opposite of those mentioned, but this logic, giving the court the opportunity to intervene, extends to 360 degrees. In this case it is even a concrete possibility for the doctor to say that the child can be saved, but the affidavit supports the opposite. Then the magistrate - we will see after what happens for the confidants in cases of urgency - when he decides? A week later? Ten days or a month later? However, it will be a decision made by a magistrate. How can one tear away from the family, the affection of parents, the possibility of healing or, in any case, a life worthy of being lived, a child and leave the decision to a court? With Amendment 3.157, because unfortunately the other has been rejected, we ask for one very simple thing; with reference to a minor, if there is a conflict between the doctor's assessment of the treatment and guidelines of the legal representative, in the absence of the early statements of treatment referred to in Article 4 - which for obvious reasons cannot be for a child of two to three months or one year or two years - the actions to protect the patient's survival prevail. In other words, you opt for life, you opt for the doctor, you give the possibility to the doctor, from the scientific, methodological point of view and of the activities that can save the child and you do not give priority to the declaration, which instead wants the child to be suppressed in the way we know, causing the food and the hydration to fail. That seems perfectly reasonable to me. This is civilization. I remember that all historical drifts, all of which were then condemned, began with such reasonings.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u411	DE BIASI (PD). Mr President, I believe that paragraph 5 of Article 3 needs to be read in its entirety, because we are discussing a particular case, namely the case where there is a conflict. There is no one here who wants to suppress anyone, but in paragraph 5 we are talking about a conflict between the legal representative of the minor person, who refuses treatment, and the doctor who considers treatment to be appropriate. When there is this conflict - since someone has said that the doctor has total power, but it is not so, because there is the trustee legal representative of the disabled or disabled person - the judge is called upon to protect and, as the text of the law states, the decision is returned to the judge to protect on appeal by the legal representative of the person concerned or the subjects referred to in Articles 406 et seq. of the civil code or by the doctor or legal representative of the health structure. Therefore, there is a plurality of subjects that can initiate recourse to this litigation. It seems to me that this constitutes a greater protection of the person than a statement of principle, which obviously, being in principle in an improper context, leaves the time it finds.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u454	Mr Giovanardi (PL-Id) Mr President, ours is a system that, even for the most trivial real estate purchase, requires the citizen to go to the notary, sign a public act and make transfers. Here we are discussing provisions on which future life, salvation, death or the lack of care of a subject depends, and I believe that the least we can ask for is that the way is guaranteed. We have already established this morning that we will have for a period of time twenty regional fora where these provisions can be deposited, plus a national seat which is equivalent. Moreover, everyone could present more than one: maybe in Tuscany, then in Puglia (if, for example, you work in Puglia), but also at national level. With reference to the Italian registers or to the work of the notaries, even for real estate that have a value of a few hundred euros, we demand the public form, the notary and the deed, while we allow for things that concern life to proceed superficially, we say preprinted. There is no limit from which to derive the true will, as stated in these provisions. That too seems to me to be a very sensible amendment.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u458	Mr Quagliarello (FL-Id-PL, PLI)). Mr President, this is another of the controversial points of this measure, which also disagrees with those who, like me, are in favour of an early declaration law. The amendment reaffirms the ban on the suspension of hydration and artificial nutrition - I would like to take a moment of attention - if this leads to the death of the patient by dehydration or malnutrition. This suspension is prohibited when death is due to these causes and not to the disease of the sick person, i.e. when the sick person, due to the suspension of nutrition or hydration, dies not because of his illness, but basically because of the lack of hydration, which clearly as death comes before that due to lack of nutrition. For this reason we ask that in this case hydration and nutrition cannot be the subject of early statements of treatment and therefore we would be included in the declarations, taking detachment from euthanasic hypotheses. Moreover, and this seems to me to be another prediction of common sense, it is required that the indication of a trust is a faculty of the patient and not an obligation, because obviously, since it is talk of self-determination, it is also possible that the patient does not want to rely on a trustee, but entrusts his statements anticipated only to his doctor.

Nice choice of civilizations. There are some very good ones, but there are also totally incompetent to enter into such delicate matters. Nice law of civilization, congratulations.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u488	MALAN (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, I am voting in favour of this amendment, which seeks to delete paragraph 5 of Article 4, which, among other things, examines the possibility of conflict between the trustee and the doctor, resolving it with the usual system referred to by Senator Giovanardi (i.e., according to paragraph 5 of Article 3, the judge decides). This is one of the fundamental principles of the measure, which leads us to vote against, which we have expressed so far and which we will continue to express. The vote could have been in favour if there had been a different attitude. In fact, it would seem obvious and elementary that, when there is someone favorable to continue treatments that allow you to keep the person alive and someone who is opposed, in doubt you choose for life. In fact - obviously - if you choose for death, the choice is irreversible, as well as - in my opinion - tends to be negative. Instead, we rely on another case, that is, the judge. Therefore the danger, which has been consolidated with the approval of Article 3 concerning the situation of minors and inability, that very similar phenomena occur, although not equal, to the case of Charlie Gard, is there. So we go against those beautiful statements that virtually all political forces issued at the time of the terrible event that Charlie Gard was about. At that time there were disdainful statements about what happened in Britain, but we are now laying the foundations for the same situation. In case of conflict, who decides? It's the judge's decision. There are so many wonderful judges who make decisions that you can well rely on. However, we do not know in the hands of those who end up. We've seen some extreme episodes. To think that the life of a family member can end up in the hands of a judge, when there is someone who strongly expresses himself in favour of keeping a loved one alive, gives the measure of why we are against the measure under consideration.

The national register serves - exactly as was done for Law No 40 of 2004 - to have a national reference point and a verification. ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u568	ROMANO (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, I think I am taking up a well-known speech which I have already outlined in the general debate. There is a substantial difference between provision and declaration: the care relationship is carried out only with the declaration, while the provision is contradictory to any form of care relationship. The provision is imperative; the declaration gives rise to an alliance and collaboration and I believe that this can be the right solution. Article 9 of the Oviedo Convention has been constantly recalled; not only that, but there are also the code of ethics and the various opinions of the National Committee for Bioethics. If there is a care report, there is only a statement; if there is no care report, then we must delete paragraph 2 of Article 1 and also amend Article 5, which speaks of shared care planning. Once again, I have to say that there is a contradiction between what is the principle of reference and the legislative reference that does not at all reflect. There is also a substantial difference between informed consent and provision in all its features and principles. It follows that, if we want to achieve an action that is truly for the good of the patient, there is only room for declaration. I know that this amendment will be rejected, but I still find it a sort of satisfaction to have witnessed from this morning what is the priority interest of the patient, without being fierce and without abandoning, in the proportionality of treatment. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII and Senators Compañera and D'Adda.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u605	Gasparri (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, these amendments call for the deletion of Article 6, which is a transitional rule. It is a dangerous rule because it says that the documents filed in the matter of health treatment at a notary or the City of Residence, before the date of entry into force of this new law, are transited into the new "Eutanasic era." Therefore, the same provisions of this law apply to these declarations. We believe, like some of the amendments by Senator Bianconi and others, that a reset should be made. At least do this. Legislation comes into force and there is this transition of declarations made at another time and in another context, perhaps without even being aware of how binding those declarations become. One made a statement ten years ago at a notary not thinking of putting himself in an infernal mechanism like this, with the children voting and the cousins and grandchildren around the deathbed, with all the absurdities voted and launched. One may have made a statement in another context. This transitional article - I say this because many people vote without reading it - says that what has been written before in another context is dealt with in the procedures of this new law, which are the ones we have illustrated. That is why the various groups' amendments call for the abolition of this transitional rule, which makes these rules on the transition to better life pass through another era, as they once said. We believe that it would be correct to say: "Lords, there is a new norm and everyone regulates himself in front of his conscience, with his family and the doctor in doing what he wants to do, given the binding nature of these new provisions. The doctor cannot intervene, the health facilities are obliged; I therefore believe that it would be wise to delete at least this rule because it would remain the planting of the law. This extension of statements made in another context is not good by thinking: Now I say it, but then I think about it and correct it. One made them in another legal context; it is truly another obnoxious to bring them into a new era. I don't know what Senator Romano or others think. This is frankly unbelievable and, therefore, I believe that voting on the deletion of this article would be wise.

ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u627	Gasparri (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, these amendments always concern the question of the repetition and eternity of the statements made previously and this is a very serious and delicate fact. There are some of these amendments from various groups: I have signed with Senators Quagliarello, Giovanardi and Senator Rizzotti Amendment 6.9, but also Senator Centinario has rightly tabled a similar amendment, as well as other Senators from other Groups. This amendment says that the documents made in the past are null and void when this law enters into force, which is exactly the opposite of what the transitional rule says. And I repeat the reasons, as I have the right to do, quietly and reasonedly, without anyone having to prevent it: it is wrong to legally translate into the new age, with different effects, different congeniences, with different obligations, with different automatisms, dear colleagues, a statement made in another context. It is true that in this House we have also stated the retroactivity of criminal rules. Can this be said or do I have to ask for a special permit? We have witnessed historical events in which the secret vote has been granted or has not been granted according to the conveniences or in which certain criminal laws have been applied retroactively. Here we are to the future application of the retroactive declaration that a person made in another context, in which he could change it, torn it and had no legal value because, since there was no law, the person who made a declaration in the past, did it for his own will, without any awareness of the rigidity and obligation that the new law, which was then not there, would have determined. It is a bit the same reasoning as the retroactivity of the penal norm, which cannot be sanctioned; here we are to the futurability of the norm on the declaration, which is placed and boxed in a different context. This is very serious, Mr President, from a moral, ethical and legal point of view, and it is a question that you should ask yourself and therefore say that things said earlier are null and void. There is a different regime and one makes a statement knowing that he puts in this infernal circuit of the doctor who doesn't count and of the relatives who vote around in bed. I know it bothers some people to say these things, but they are the pure and simple truths. This amendment would remove the counter: the legal regime is different and the person who makes the declaration, if he wants to do it, knows that it fits into this tunnel, where he or no one else will decide no more. This is a legal principle and see that the parallel with the retroactivity of the criminal law fits absolutely. Here we are faced with an inverse problem: we move into a different legal regime a statement made without knowing that there were all these constraints. This is crazy! I hope there are very few statements, because then the beauty of these laws is that they are invoked by many, but then they are used little (just go and see those on civil unions or other laws how much they have been used). So, you make laws for a few who are also wrong. I believe that if a law is right, it must also be done for one; if it is wrong and it is for a few, I believe that this is a reason for voting even more strongly in favour of this amendment. And there is the concern to reiterate how one can go even to a notary. Be careful, colleagues on the left, you who are so sensitive to Professor Javazzi's liberalism, will come the usual article against the notaries, valued by this provision. Mine wants to be not a joke, but the sense of how absurd it is to foresee this regime as a transitional rule. In addition, I have never been as enthusiastic about federalism as the excellent President of our session, but this federalism dissiancantesi about more than 8,000 municipalities in the matter of life and death makes me think that, although stunned in times, ways and procedures, that attempt to adjust the omelette through the regional institute, which I even less loved than the municipal one, has its foundation. ParlaMint-IT_2017-12-13-LEG17-Senato-sed-916.ana.u650	MAURO Mario (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, I would like to give the reasons for Amendment 7.2, which, moreover, is simply deleted from Article 7, with a substantive reflection which I think can also be used to re-evaluate some of the work we have done and which has seen as a reference reason in many of the quotations made by colleagues who have opposed this rule that this is a bad rule. Why is that a bad norm? In the meantime, because much of what is presented as a novelty is an already seen: already today there is the appropriate discipline on informed consent, as confirmed by the 2008 Constitutional Court's ruling No 438, by Article 3 of the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union and by Title IV of the Code of Medical Ethics. Already today is confirmed the full legal validity of the DAT (judgment of the Court of Cassation n. 2174 of 2007) and already today it is possible to appoint a trustee, as provided for in article 1, paragraph 40, of the law n. 76 of 2016. Any form of therapeutic aggression is already prohibited, as provided for in Law No. 24 of 2017 and Article 16 of the Code of Medical Deontology. So what's the real news? The real novelties that have been proposed and almost imposed on this House concern, for example, the elimination of the conscientious objection of the doctor, the replacement of medical professionalism with a cross-cutting role, once again, of the judiciary, an out-of-the-box impact on the role of family members and - as has already been repeated several times - a systematic attack on what we can call the therapeutic alliance. In short, a concept of a liberal brand. The reflections proposed, therefore, of those who consider the norm a step forward of civilization, are actually to be reversed in the light of this simple consideration: there is behind a conception of the State for which the State is everything and the person is nothing. In this sense, the deleterious amendments have the function of curbing such reasoning.
